The daily field guide for digital media

AI Media Scout

2026-11-23 · All platforms · Open-source software

The edition has 87 complete guides and 19 screened discoveries. It completes 15 earlier pending guides and details 31 substantive release updates. Today’s research covers editable transcripts and chapters, writable MIDI handoffs, multi-view scene capture and motion transfer. Blender 5.3, released November 10, supports native Gaussian splat import and rendering. Preserve the original splat for handoff because native export is unavailable in that version. XR Blocks v0.25 documents a separate WebGPU route for interactive splat rendering. SOMA 1.0 and QtMeshEditor 3.72 document distinct editable assembly and character capture handoffs. Code grants were read in full. Models, services, creative inputs and hardware or software hosts have separate requirements. This edition is based on source review and published documentation. No discovered app was installed or run. The expanded plan covered 122 fields, 1408/1408 repository-query attempts and 37 model tasks. The repository lane recorded 1406 successful, 2 partial and 0 failed queries, with 257 bounded. The model lane recorded 0 failed tasks. Metadata from 2,887 saved repository pages included 84,375 numeric identities. The complete retained backlog spans 121,834 candidates, including pilot history and 1,070 previously screened guides still pending before this edition. Creative relevance and the complete software grant were checked before including any discovery. Unreviewed and excluded records stay in local research. Live primary research yielded 374 complete usable responses from 460 distinct URL attempts, including a non-GitHub source in each of 122 fields. 4 captures reached the size bound and 82 other sources were inaccessible or unusable. The ecosystem checks cover all 8 configured groups and record available findings and gaps. Evidence labels in this edition are 0 Recommended, 0 Ready to try, 93 Quality unverified, 13 Experimental. Repository searches returned no saved rows for Camera-reactive projection installations, Editable AI transcript chapters and audio publication, Live production accessibility and routed captions, Learned material capture and de-lighting, Learned projector compensation and exhibit color, Neural splat compositing and editor interchange. Primary web research for these fields is included in the coverage notes. Updated evidence labels for previous entries: See2Sound Backend (Quality unverified to Experimental), Cypher DJ (Quality unverified to Experimental), SOMA (Experimental to Quality unverified), OpenGPEX (Quality unverified to Experimental), AtlasForge (Quality unverified to Experimental), OpenFill (Quality unverified to Experimental). 33 model tasks reached the configured response limit of 50 rows. A model record remains a discovery lead until code and use are verified. Model tasks with no rows: text2text-generation, zero-shot-audio-classification, audio-segmentation, image-matting. The separate watchlist made 66 unsuccessful repository checks. Collection logged 5 enrichment failures. Credential-shaped material in unsealed captures was removed before sealing. The affected source origins and before-and-after hashes remain in private evidence.

122 creative fields; 1408/1408 repository queries attempted; 37/37 model-task queries attempted. 85283 distinct source candidates and 1489 model leads. 87 detailed profiles and 19 additional screened discoveries. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

Profiles are based on current primary documentation. Creative quality and performance claims are attributed to the developers unless hands-on testing is explicitly recorded. Unknown hardware requirements stay unknown. Code, model weights, hosted services, and required proprietary hosts can have different terms.

Scope: Create a small vector illustration, inspect paths and grouping, then export an SVG master and a PNG preview.

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Live-document editing has separate managed-session prerequisites. Check the working copy and preview before writing back.

How it uses AI

The Rust MCP server exposes typed editing, inspection and export operations in Inkscape to a connected AI agent, preserving editable SVG geometry. [1] [4] [5]

Introduction

The Rust MCP server exposes typed editing, inspection and export operations in Inkscape to a connected AI agent, preserving editable SVG geometry. [1] [4] [5]

What it is good for

Create a small vector illustration, inspect paths and grouping, then export an SVG master and a PNG preview. [1] [4] [5]

Demo & examples

The author provides editable SVG masters and a five-task report with the images rendered through the MCP workflow. [1] [4] [5]

Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [1] [4] [5]

  1. Choose the current macOS Manager release or the documented source build.
  2. Select one supported MCP client and a dedicated SVG workspace, then reconnect the client.
  3. Check the candidate distribution's signing and verification evidence and open a small working copy.

First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [1] [4] [5]

  1. Author a few editable shapes and group them by their intended meaning.
  2. Inspect paths and render a preview before committing changes to the selected drawing.
  3. Save an SVG master and a PNG preview, then inspect the named groups in Inkscape.

The current Manager and source bootstrap documents target Apple Silicon macOS. Equivalent Windows or Linux Manager packages are undocumented.

  • Commercial: Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.

How it uses AI

  • Weights: The application is MIT. Optional models, original recordings and lyrics have separate terms.
  • Commercial: Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
  • Cost: The documented local aligning path needs device compute and model downloads. Optional configured providers keep their own billing.

Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [1] [4] [5] [6]

Limitations

The 51-song author evaluation used intentionally shortened lyrics. Its higher 0.51 figure uses idealized verse ordering supplied from reference timestamps. Assess the real provider, language and vocal mix separately. [1] [4] [5] [6]

How it uses AI

MVD-Fusion generates correlated RGB and depth views from a single RGB reference by conditioning a diffusion network on projected depth. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Introduction

MVD-Fusion generates correlated RGB and depth views from a single RGB reference by conditioning a diffusion network on projected depth. [1] [3] [4] [5]

What it is good for

  3. Use an official evaluation preset before adapting a reference image interface.

The guide estimates an initial Kokoro download near 350 MB. RAM, VRAM and total storage for the first-use workflow are undocumented.
Software
Python 3.12–3.13, Kokoro runtime and FFmpeg for the selected output. Other voices and OCR need extras.
Platforms
The maintainer documents cross-platform document conversion and a Docker option. Apple Vision OCR is macOS-only.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [2] [1] [4] [5]

  • Weights: MIT project code. Kokoro-82M has an Apache-2.0 model grant. Optional Piper and Chatterbox components have separate licenses.
  • Commercial: Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
  • Cost: Local synthesis with a one-time model download. The scoped localhost route requires no hosted API.

Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [1] [4] [5]

Limitations

Scans require the supported OCR route. Engines without token timings use sentence-level estimates. The public package still declares Beta. [1] [4] [5]

  3. Inspect the result from several views and preserve the original plus an interchange export.

Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [1] [4] [5] [6]

Hardware
NVIDIA compute capability 7.5 or newer is the documented baseline. Exact VRAM, RAM and storage for the selected scene are undocumented.
Software
C++23 and CUDA 12.8+ for source builds, with NVIDIA drivers 570 or newer. Model downloads and optional Blender integration have their own dependencies.
Platforms
Windows and Linux source builds are documented. Windows is the primary prebuilt target. No macOS GPU training route is documented.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [2] [1] [4] [5] [6]

  • Weights: The complete GPLv3 text and NVIDIA library permission were reviewed. The project declares GPLv3 or later. Read the third-party notices for CUDA components and downloaded weighting models.
  • Commercial: Project code is governed by GPL-3.0. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
  • Cost: Source builds are available under GPL. The distributor uses paid Windows binary access. Local training uses compatible GPU hardware.

Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [1] [4] [5] [6]

Limitations

Source camera quality, overdraw, color space and motion affect results. Keep the original export if rendering the scene in a host without splat export. VR and segmentation features have additional dependencies. [1] [4] [5] [6]

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. The current schema has explicit musical and import constraints. Live audio delivery differs from score editing and is platform-bound.

How it uses AI

Skitz connects an external model agent to revision-bound editable music scores, inspection and output, including notation and audio render tools. [1] [4] [5]

Introduction

Skitz connects an external model agent to revision-bound editable music scores, inspection and output, including notation and audio render tools. [1] [4] [5]

What it is good for

Draft a short editable arrangement and make a bounded revision while protecting an existing voice or track. [1] [4] [5]

Demo & examples

The source includes an authored fixture and a workflow for generating PDF, MIDI, MusicXML and WAV artifacts. Its notation and rendering claims are version- and platform-scoped. [1] [4] [5]

Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [1] [4] [5]

  1. Prepare the documented Python, Node, npm and uv environment with the reviewed dependencies.
  2. Configure the selected compatible model provider or use the explicitly separate local score tools.
  3. Check LilyPond, FluidSynth and the selected SoundFont for its documented macOS PDF/WAV path.

First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [1] [4] [5]

  1. Create a short score and mark a voice protected.
  2. Ask for a limited revision, then inspect the exact score diff and run the supplied checks.
  3. Export the canonical editable score and any supported render artifacts, preserving the project version.

Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [1] [4] [5]

Hardware
Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
Software
Python 3.11+, Node.js 22.8+, npm and uv. Engraving and synthesis have extra LilyPond, FluidSynth and SoundFont requirements.
Platforms
The source explicitly says the current PDF/WAV worker is supported on the tested macOS path. Source, MIDI and MusicXML can work separately where dependencies are available.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [2] [1] [4] [5]

  • Weights: Full MIT source grant, with inherited notices retained. The model provider, separately obtained SoundFonts and supplied music sources have distinct terms.
  • Commercial: Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
  • Cost: Editable local score commands do not need inference. The selected agent uses its own subscription, quota or billed API.

Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [1] [4] [5]

Limitations

The current schema has explicit musical and import constraints. Live audio delivery differs from score editing and is platform-bound. [1] [4] [5]

FFmpeg Forge CLI

Documented creative workflow · GPL-3.0 · recently-created

This completes an earlier pending guide with a current source review.

Scope: Compare a small restored excerpt from owned footage with its source and retain an independent archival copy.

Evidence checked 2026-11-23; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: Reviewed the complete project software grant and its redistribution conditions. Model, data and service terms are scoped separately. [2]

  • Reproducible setup · documented: The installation and first-use steps are in the cited documentation. Reproduction of this workflow remains unverified. [1] [4]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: The reviewed sources do not establish workflow-specific support, fixes and unresolved blockers.
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand use of the exact workflow was not established in this source review.
  • Models, services and costs · documented: The reviewed CLI software grant is GPL-3.0. FFmpeg builds, shader/HRTF components, model code and weight artifacts retain their own terms. Local processing takes device resources. The learned runtime downloads can occupy several GB. [1] [4]

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Native encoder, learned upscaling and restoration options vary by hardware. Do not treat generated detail as preserved information.

How it uses AI

The command-line workflow offers separately installed learned restoration routes, including ONNX denoising and selected video super-resolution engines. [1] [4]

Introduction

The command-line workflow offers separately installed learned restoration routes, including ONNX denoising and selected video super-resolution engines. [1] [4]

What it is good for

Compare a small restored excerpt from owned footage with its source and retain an independent archival copy. [1] [4]

Demo & examples

The maintainer lists learned upscaling and denoising choices, codec paths and command examples. Temporal consistency and preservation quality depend on the selected engine. [1] [4]

Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [1] [4]

  1. Pick a documented CLI release or source build for the intended operating system.
  2. Confirm the particular learned engine's Windows support, prerequisites and upstream weight terms before installing.
  3. Use the maintained dependency notices to check the codecs and other components for the selected workflow.

First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [1] [4]

  1. Cut a short excerpt to evaluate with the selected engine.
  2. Compare grain, edge artifacts, motion, color and audio alignment with the unmodified original.
  3. Write an output to a separate path and retain original footage and the reviewed settings.

Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [1] [4]

Hardware
Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
Software
The source documents C# 5 and the .NET Framework 4.0 profile, Windows build tooling or Mono for a base source build. The learned Windows options have separate runtime requirements.
Platforms
The CLI repository documents Windows builds and Linux or macOS CLI source compilation. Its optional learned engine installation instructions specifically target Windows.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [2] [1] [4]

  • Weights: The reviewed CLI software grant is GPL-3.0. FFmpeg builds, shader/HRTF components, model code and weight artifacts retain their own terms.
  • Commercial: Project code is governed by GPL-3.0. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
  • Cost: Local processing takes device resources. The learned runtime downloads can occupy several GB.

Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [1] [4]

Native encoder, learned upscaling and restoration options vary by hardware. Do not treat generated detail as preserved information. [1] [4]

Hudka

Documented creative workflow · MIT · first-profile

First complete guide in the library. The publication and documentation dates are not necessarily the project’s launch date.

Docs checked 2026-11-23 · 1 GitHub stars · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Build a draft sound-effects track for an owned short video, revise individual cue timing and verify the license report before handoff.

Evidence checked 2026-11-23; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: Reviewed the complete project software grant and its redistribution conditions. Model, data and service terms are scoped separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: The maintainer publishes a cue-sheet workflow and an RTX 4070 timing estimate for a 48-second clip. The documented test scope is Windows/NVIDIA. [1] [3] [4]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: The installation and first-use steps are in the cited documentation. Reproduction of this workflow remains unverified. [1] [3] [4]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: The reviewed sources do not establish workflow-specific support, fixes and unresolved blockers.
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand use of the exact workflow was not established in this source review.
  • Models, services and costs · documented: The complete Hudka code grant is MIT. The selected gated model terms and input/voice/media rights are separate. Review the scope of the generated license report before use. The selected local model route uses storage and device compute. The optional contact-sheet analysis sends frames to a configured hosted model and can incur charges. [1] [3] [4]

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Automatic cues need human review and use alternate source clips only with permission. The two documented alternate engines are explicitly untested.

How it uses AI

Hudka combines scene/motion analysis with local learned sound and music generation, then mixes timed cues alongside original dialogue. The editable cue sheet can be designed locally or with an optional model that reviews contact sheets. [1] [3] [4]

Introduction

Hudka combines scene/motion analysis with local learned sound and music generation, then mixes timed cues alongside original dialogue. The editable cue sheet can be designed locally or with an optional model that reviews contact sheets. [1] [3] [4]

What it is good for

Build a draft sound-effects track for an owned short video, revise individual cue timing and verify the license report before handoff. [1] [3] [4]

Demo & examples

The maintainer publishes a cue-sheet workflow and an RTX 4070 timing estimate for a 48-second clip. The documented test scope is Windows/NVIDIA. [1] [3] [4]

Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Review the named Windows source and setup path and current FFmpeg and uv requirements.
  2. Read the selected gated Stability Audio model card and terms before obtaining the weights.
  3. Verify the chosen host runtime and storage path, then begin with a short permitted clip.

First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Generate or author a small editable cue sheet using the local route.
  2. Render several candidate effects and check placement and loudness against important original speech.
  3. Make a limited cue revision, rerender, and retain the original source, cue file and license report.

Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [1] [3] [4]

Hardware
The author estimates about 5 GB working storage and reports 1.7 GB peak VRAM on an RTX 4070 test. CPU mode is documented with different processing time.
Software
The source documents a Windows setup with Python managed by uv, FFmpeg/PySceneDetect and separately acquired audio models.
Platforms
The diagnosed desktop path covers Windows. The author says Linux/macOS need adaptations for bundle launch and drive detection.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [2] [1] [3] [4]

  • Weights: The complete Hudka code grant is MIT. The selected gated model terms and input/voice/media rights are separate. Review the scope of the generated license report before use.
  • Commercial: Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
  • Cost: The selected local model route uses storage and device compute. The optional contact-sheet analysis sends frames to a configured hosted model and can incur charges.

Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [1] [3] [4]

Limitations

Automatic cues need human review and use alternate source clips only with permission. The two documented alternate engines are explicitly untested. [1] [3] [4]

Repository · Documentation

Primary sources

  1. Official project documentation
  2. Complete software license reviewed
  3. Engine and media terms inventory
  4. Primary Stable Audio sound-effects model card
